## Document 003: 10月FOMC議事要旨：12月利上げ視野、少数ながら50bp議論 ～FRBは2027年のインフレ鈍化シナリオを維持～

Source PDF: all report pdf/10月FOMC議事要旨：12月利上げ視野、少数ながら50bp議論   ～FRBは2027年のインフレ鈍化シナリオを維持～.pdf
Report source: DLRI
Date: 2026-11-19
Pages: 4
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

FED  Watching

2026 年 11 月 19 日

1/4 
10 月 FOMC 議事要旨：12 月利上げ視野、少数ながら 50bp 議論 
～FRB は 2027 年のインフレ鈍化シナリオを維持～ 
 経済調査部 主任エコノミスト  桂畑 誠治  
（℡：050-5474-7493）

要旨  
・12 月利上げ再開がメインシナリオ：10 月 27〜28 日開催のFOMC議事要旨が公表された。
同会合では政策金利の据え置きが決定されたものの、参加者のほぼ全員が「今後数カ月間（12 月
15〜16 日会合を含む）での利上げ再開」を基本路線として維持していることが確認された。

・タカ派的テールリスクの浮上：一方で、2〜3 名の参加者がデータ次第で「50bpの大幅利上
げ」を検討する余地に言及しており、市場ではタカ派的なテールリスクが意識される内容となっ
た。

・2027 年のインフレ鈍化見通しは不変：FRBのベースライン（基本見通し）では、インフレは
目先高止まりするものの、関税影響の減退やエネルギー・AI関連の供給制約緩和に伴い、2027
年にかけて鈍化し、時間の経過とともに目標の 2％へ収束していくとの見方は崩れていない。

1．新体制下での情報発信と議事 要旨 の重要性  
新体制移行後、会合直後の声明文は大幅に簡素化され、議長の記者会見も短縮されるなど、直
近の直接的な情報発信は絞り込まれている。その分、後日公表される議事要旨は高い情報量を維
持しており、FRBの本音や委員会内部の議論の分布を推し量る最重要ツールとしての重みが増して
いる。もっとも、公表がFOMC終了から 3 週間後となるため、その間に発表された最新データとの
タイムラグ（情報の遅効性）を考慮して評価する必要がある。

2．10 月会合 の政策 判断 と金融 政策 の基本 スタンス  
10 月会合では、FF レート誘導目標レンジ（3.75％〜4.00％）の据え置きが決定された（投票権
を持つ委員ベースでは 12 名中 10 名が賛成、2 名が反対）。据え置きを支持した委員は、「さらなる
情報の蓄積が、インフレ率を 2％の目標へ適時に戻すための適切な政策スタンスを見極める上で役
立つ」と指摘した。今回の据え置きは利上げサイクルの終了ではなく、累積的な引き締め効果と最
新データを確認するための「一時停止（スキップ）」という位置づけである。

### Page 2

2/4 
今後の政策決定については、「新たに入手されるデータ、変化する見通し、リスクバランスに依存
する」とのデータ次第（データ・ディペンデント）の姿勢を強調した。その上で、基本見通しの下
では「9 月の経済・金利予測（SEP：議長自身は今回も提出せず）とおおむね整合的な形で、今後数
カ月間（12 月 15〜16 日の次回会合など）で追加利上げを行うことが適切となる可能性が高い」と
の見解で、参加者のほぼ全員が一致した。

3．経済・物価・雇用の現状認識と 中長期 の見通し  
委員会全体の共通認識を確認すると、景気については「底堅い家計支出と堅調な設備投資に支え
られ、経済活動は引き続き力強いペースで拡大している」との見方で一致した。 
労働市場については「安定しており、最大雇用に近づいている」と判断され、目先も安定した状
態が続き、雇用の上振れリスクと下振れリスクはおおむね均衡していると評価された。 
インフレに関しては、依然として高止まりしており、物価上昇圧力は経済全体に広範に及んでい
るとの指摘が相次いだ。特に高いエネルギー価格や精製燃料の供給制約が足元の押し上げ要因とな
っており、見通しには上振れリスクが意識されている。一方、名目賃金の伸びは緩やかな水準にと
どまり、2％のインフレ目標とおおむね整合的と判断された。 
中長期のインフレ見通しでは、9 月コア CPI（前月比＋0.3％）の上振れを受けても、スタッフの
インフレ予測は 9 月会合時点からほとんど変更されず、参加者の間でも従来の基本見通しが維持さ
れた。大半の参加者は、インフレ率は目先高止まりするものの、関税引き上げに伴う価格転嫁圧力
の減退や、エネルギー価格・AI 関連投資に伴う供給制約（電力・インフラ需要等）の緩和により、
時間の経過とともに（9 月 SEP で示された 2027 年の鈍化軌道に沿って）2％に向けて低下していく
と予想した。中長期的なインフレ期待も引き続き 2％と整合的であり、これが過度な利上げ回数の
積み増し（ターミナルレートの大幅引き上げ）を抑制する根拠となっている。

4．タカ派サプライズ：「 12 月 50bp 利上げ」への言及と今後の焦点  
今回の議事要旨における最大の注目点（タカ派サプライズ）は、9 月 SEP に沿った追加利上げ（25bp
ずつ）というメインシナリオの裏で、大幅利上げの選択肢が議論されていたことである。「新たに入
手されるインフレデータが上振れれば、次回 12 月会合で 25bp を超える利上げ（50bp など）が検討
に値する」と発言した参加者が 2〜3 名いたことが明らかとなった。 
議事要旨の公表前にあたる11月10日に発表された10月コアCPIは前月比＋0.3％と3カ月連続
で市場予想を上回り、3 カ月年率換算ペースでは目標を大幅に上回る高い伸びとなった。さらに、
10 月会合では据え置きに賛成したウォラーFRB 理事も、10 月 CPI の結果を受けて「50bp の利上げ
を検討すべき」との趣旨の発言を行っており、同理事は議事要旨内の「タカ派の少数意見」に含ま
れていたとみられる。

5．マーケットへのインプリケーション  
現時点では 50bp 利上げは少数意見にとどまるが、FRB が「インフレ再燃リスクに対して後手に回

がある。仮に市場金利の低下などによって金融環境の引き締まりが緩めば、利上げペースの加速に
追い込まれる可能性が高い。
12 月 FOMC に向けては、今後発表される 11 月の雇用統計、CPI、ISM 景気指数の結果次第で、市
場の織り込みが「25bp 利上げ」から「50bp 利上げ」へと急速に傾くリスクがある。債券市場ではタ
ーミナルレートの上振れを意識する形で米 2 年債利回りに上昇圧力がかかりやすく、為替市場でも
ドル高・円安地合いが継続しやすい展開が見込まれる。

### Page 2

2  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/
上週重要市場資訊
 歐洲企業協會(Business Europe)發布一項調查顯示，自 6月以來，歐洲企業對工
業和服務業前景的信心有所改善，惟對通膨上升和主權債務問題的擔憂日益加
劇。由於美伊戰爭推高能源成本，而歐盟絕大部分能源依賴進口，能源價格上漲
進一步傳導至零售價格，導致今年通膨升至歐洲央行 2%目標水準之上，市場預
期歐洲央行將於 12月再度升息。
 美國舊金山聯準銀行總裁戴利(Mary Daly)表示，價格壓力正從能源和商品擴散
至服務業，而服務業通膨可能非常頑固。潛在通膨可能並未像市場預期的降
溫。近期能源價格回落，拉低整體通膨，然而核心通膨仍持續往上，聯準會需
要進一步升息以應對居高不下的通膨。此外，聯準會理事克里斯沃勒
(Christopher Waller)及達拉斯聯準銀行總裁洛根(Lorie Logan)均提出升息兩碼的
可能性，多位聯準會官員頻頻釋放偏鷹言論，升息預期升溫，預計將支撐美元

走勢。
 日本 10 月企業商品物價指數年增率由前月的 6.8%上升至 7.1%，主因為中東衝
突及 AI相關需求推升能源和晶片價格，通膨持續上行，提供日本升息有利條件，
有利日幣走升。
 美國 10月 CPI月增率 0.4%、核心 CPI月增率 0.3%雙雙高於預期，10月 PPI年
增率亦升至 6.5%，為四年來新高，通膨居高不下，FedWatch 顯示 12 月升息機
率達九成，2027年 1 月再升息一碼機率逾六成。
 台灣 10月出口 967.6 億美元，為史上單月首度突破 900億美元，年增率 58.9%
為連續 36個月正成長，創下史上最長成長周期。

### Page 3

3  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
 美元兌新台幣匯率走勢  
Nov/16/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
    上週台幣匯價高點為 32.794，低點在 32.994，以 32.838 作收，較前一週收盤價
貶值 7 分。 
    上週台幣走貶，週初，台股反彈，台幣一度走升至 32.794週內高點，不過，外
資延續匯出走勢，台幣隨即收斂所有漲幅，並貶回 32.950 上方；週間，美國公布 10
月 CPI數據表現優於預期，數據強化 12月升息一碼的預期，同時也開始反應升息兩
碼的風險，美元指數強勢突破 103 整數關卡，美元利差優勢擴大，台幣承壓走低至
32.994週內低點；臨近週末，台股走揚，外資轉為買超台股並匯入，台幣升破 32.830，
隨後匯價逢低吸引進口商買盤進場，終場在央行調節之下收在 32.838。

＜未來分析＞ 
     預估本週台幣走勢偏弱。就國際市場而言，美國 10月 CPI優於市場預期，顯
示通膨壓力仍在，市場預期聯準會 12月升息一碼，也不排除一口氣升息兩碼，美元
利差優勢將不利台幣表現；就台灣市場來看，上週公佈台灣 10月出口 967.6億美元，
是史上單月首度突破 900 億美元，寫下單月新高，且年增率 58.9%創下連續 36紅的
表現。基本面強勁，將有助於台幣的表現；盤勢上來看，輝達股價下跌風波，連帶
影響台股表現，整體外資仍偏向匯出，台幣仍面臨貶值的壓力。綜合以上，預估本
週台幣走勢偏弱。 
＜預估本週＞ 美元兌台幣：32.600-33.200

### Page 4

4  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
美元兌人民幣匯率走勢  
Nov/16/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
上週人民幣區間震盪。週初，川普表示若伊朗願意簽署具有實質內容的協議，
美方就不太可能重新展開大規模的攻擊，帶動市場進一步降低戰事升溫的預期，油
價下跌，風險偏好回升，減弱美元避險需求，人民幣受益走升至 6.6650；週間，聯
準會官員沃勒鷹派發言，加以美國 10月 CPI表現優於預期，帶動美債殖利率及美元
指數走揚人民幣走貶至 6.6800；臨近週末，美國 10 月 PPI 年增率 6.5%，創下 2022
年 11 月以來高點，顯示通膨壓力難以消退，美元指數高檔震盪，人民幣於 6.6700上
方走勢偏弱，終場境外人民幣收在 6.6746，週線貶值 10點，境內人民幣則收在 6.6726，
週線升值 37點。

＜未來分析＞ 
     預估本週人民幣區間震盪。就國際市場來看，美國 10月 CPI優於市場預期，
強化市場對於聯準會 12月升息的預期，利差因素支撐美元指數反彈，將抑制人民幣
走升；就中國國內市場來看，中國最新公布 10 月貿易順差規模持續超過 1000 億美
元，出口商結匯需求將緩解人民幣貶值壓力；最後就技術面來看，USD/CNH日線圖
中，USD/CNH重返五日均線之上，顯示人民幣強勢升值的趨勢暫歇，綜合以上，預
估本週人民幣區間震盪。

＜預估本週＞ 美元兌人民幣：6.6600-6.6900

### Page 5

5  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
美元兌日圓匯率走勢  
         Nov/16/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
上週日圓走弱。週初，USD/JPY 在 158 整數關卡附近波動；週間，美國 10 月
CPI數據表現優於預期，且多位聯準會官員發表鷹派言論，帶動美元指數回升至 102
以上，USD/JPY 上行突破 160 整數關卡；臨近週末，美國 10 月 PPI 年增率 6.5%為
四年來新高，USD/JPY持穩於高位，終場收在 159.66，週線上漲 121 點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
  預估本週 USD/JPY 區間震盪，主要原因如下：一、日本公布 10 月企業商品物
價指數年增率由前月的 6.8%上升至 7.1%，主因為中東衝突及 AI相關需求推升能源
和晶片價格，通膨持續上行，提供日本升息有利條件，預計將支撐日幣走勢。二、
美國 10月 CPI月增率 0.4%、核心 CPI月增率 0.3%皆高於預期，10 月 PPI 亦升至高
位，加之多位聯準會官員鷹派發言，FEDWATCH顯示 12月升息機率已上升至 90%，
明年 1 月再升息一碼機率也超過 60%，美元持穩於 102以上高位，將有利 USD/JPY
走勢。三、日本財務大臣片山皋月表示近期匯市走勢出現單邊快速波動，日本政府
將繼續與美國財政部密切合作，並採取適當應對措施，日本政府逢高採取干預將限
縮 USD/JPY上行空間。四、就技術面而言，在日線圖中，USD/JPY突破七周來的主
要交易區間以及年線壓力，在 160 整數關口徘徊，短線多方動能較佔優勢，然而匯
價來至高點有日本政府干預風險，預期將限縮上行幅度。綜合以上，預估 USD/JPY
本週呈現區間震盪的可能性較高。

## Document 077: 2026年11月LPR报价保持稳定，短期内下调可能性不大

Source PDF: all report pdf/2026年11月LPR报价保持稳定，短期内下调可能性不大.pdf
Report source: 东方金诚
Date: 2026-11-20
Pages: 2
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

东方金诚宏观研究

1 
2026年 11月 LPR报价保持稳定，短期内下调可能性不大 
 王青  冯琳 
2026年 11月 20日，中国人民银行授权全国银行间同业拆借中心公布新版 LPR报价：1
年期品种报 3.0%，上月为 3.0%；5年期以上品种报 3.5%，上月为 3.5%。 
解读：

11月两个期限品种的 LPR报价保持不变，符合市场预期。11月初，央行优化短端利率调
控机制，宣布自 11月 2日起每日开展固定利率的隔夜逆回购操作招标，并明确目前操作利率
是 1.25%；7天期逆回购操作调整为多重价位中标，政策利率属性淡化。这意味着，央行主要
政策利率从 7天期向隔夜“换锚”。“换锚”后，DR001 的走廊区间从 1.15%-1.65%下调至
1.0%-1.5%。但需要指出的是，这并非政策利率下调，也意味着 11月 LPR报价的定价基础没
有变化。另外，尽管近期 DR001 等主要货币市场利率、银行同业存单到期收益率、存款利率
都有不同程度的下行，但受贷款利率持续走低等影响，当前银行净息差仍然处在历史低位附
近，报价行缺乏主动下调 LPR报价加点的动力。 
近期 LPR报价保持稳定，根本原因是三季度 GDP增速回升到 4.5%，出口延续高增，以高
技术制造业为代表的新质生产力领域加速发展；10 月宏观数据有所走弱，但更多属于月度正
常波动，四季度经济增长动能仍有上行趋势，完成全年“4.5%至 5.0%”的经济增长目标难度
不大。在此背景下，近期包括全面降息在内的总量货币政策保持定力。 
展望未来，短期内 LPR报价下调的可能性不大。主要原因在于，9月 29日央行调整完善
若干结构性货币政策工具，包括 PSL 利率下调 0.25 个百分点，支农支小再贷款额度上调
5000亿，其中民企再贷款额度上调 3000亿，科技创新和技术改造再贷款额度上调 2000亿，
“报销”比例由原来的 60%上调至 100%；与此同时，财政部门也会同央行、金融监管总局，
首次在全国范围内出台了针对部分首套刚需购房贷款的贴息政策。这意味着逆周期调节已优
先选择结构性政策工具发力。我们判断，在以上结构性稳增长、稳楼市措施出台后，接下来
将进入一段政策效果观察期，短期内全面降息等总量货币政策工具跟进的可能性下降，LPR
报价的定价基础会保持稳定。 
拉长时间来看，综合考虑经济金融演化趋势，预计 2027年央行有望全面降息 10到 20个

### Page 2

东方金诚宏观研究

2 
基点（0.1到 0.2个百分点），届时将引导 LPR报价跟进下调。

2 群益投顧 
  ESG 
廣達積極推動環境、社會及公司治理（ESG）永續發展，並將節能減碳、人
才培育及供應鏈管理納入企業營運策略。環境方面，公司於 2024 年通過科
學基礎減碳目標倡議（SBTi）審查，承諾以 2022 年為基準，2030 年前將範
疇一及範疇二溫室氣體排放量降低 42%、範疇三降低 25%，並以 2050 年達
成淨零排放為長期目標。公司亦持續推動再生能源使用及能源管理，2024 年
綠電使用占總用電量 47.51%，並設定 2030 年再生能源使用比例達 50%的目
標。 
社會責任方面，廣達重視員工權益、職業安全及人才培育，遵循責任商業聯
盟（ RBA）行為準則，並透過廣達文教基金會及廣藝基金會推動教育與藝文
活動。公司治理方面，廣達持續強化風險管理、資訊安全及供應鏈永續管理，
並參照 TCFD 及 SASB 等國際框架揭露永續資訊，以提升營運透明度及長期
經營韌性。 
公司簡介 
廣達電腦（Quanta Computer Inc.， 2382 TT）成立於 1988 年，總部位於台灣
桃園，為全球主要電子產品原廠設計製造（ODM）廠商之一，主要業務涵蓋
筆記型電腦、伺服器、雲端運算設備及其他智慧電子產品。公司早期以筆記
型電腦代工為主要業務，憑藉產品研發、供應鏈管理及全球製造能力，建立
與國際品牌客戶的長期合作關係，近年則積極擴展雲端運算及人工智慧相
關業務。 
廣達透過旗下雲達科技（QCT）布局資料中心市場，提供伺服器、儲存設備、
網路交換器及整機櫃解決方案，並隨大型雲端服務供應商（CSP）擴 大 AI 基
礎建設投資，逐步深化 AI 伺服器業務布局。相較傳統筆電代工，AI 伺服器
對系統整合、散熱、電源管理及高速互連技術要求較高，帶動產品複雜度及
單機價值提升，成為公司營收成長的重要驅動因素。 
展望未來，廣達持續推進 AI 伺服器及機櫃級系統整合能力，並透過全球生
產據點布局，強化供應鏈韌性及在地化交付能力。隨 AI 運算需求持續擴張，
伺服器業務可望維持公司中長期成長動能，惟仍須關注 CSP 資本支出、產
品轉換時程及新平台量產效率對營運的影響。

3 群益投顧 
ASP 提升驅動營收增長，造成毛利率稀釋(3)通用型伺服器出貨量小幅季增，
且 ASP 提升。綜上原因導致 3Q26 毛利率季減 0.27ppts，帶動營益率季減
0.41ppts，此一情景預估將延續至 2027 年，且我們預估 Vera Rubin 世代平
台 ASP 較 GB300 系列接近倍增，雖營收與獲利將可持續上升，但未來 margin
將持續承壓。雖目前有數個專案採用 Consignment model，然而主要以通用
型伺服器與 ASIC 平台為主，且出貨形式由 L6 至 L11 皆有，營收貢獻相對
於採用 B/S 的整櫃 GPU 伺服器而言佔比極低。據我們了解目前已有確定一
個 Vera Rubin 的大型專案及數個規模較小的專案將採用 Consignment 
model，但預估占整體伺服器營收比重仍低，對於毛利率的改善效果有限。
由於 B/S 將稀釋毛利率，Consignment 則會使營收下滑，因此未來針對 ODM
的獲利能力的評估重點應轉向以營業利益年複合成長率(CAGR)或是 ROIC
為主。 
終止華亞科技園區收購，2028 年產能擴充計畫維持不變：廣達表示，由於
對廠方規格及交付時程等事項無法取得共識，因此撤銷友達華亞科技園區
廠房的收購案，原先規劃的產能將透過海外投資計畫或其他彈性應變方案，
維持 2028 年投產的計畫不變。廣達 3Q26 資本支出 128 億元，2026 前三季
累計資本支出達 346 億元，預估 2027 年資本支出介於 400-450 億元，投資
金額成長幅度收斂，將主要用於改良租賃廠房及電力升級。管理層表示 AI
擴廠計畫為配合 CSP 客戶未來 12-24 個月的需求規劃，且伺服器屬於客製
化接單生產(Build-to-Order)，非承擔庫存風險的標準品倍或生產，資本支出
主要投入的項目為廠房電力設施及高階冷卻系統等通用的基礎設施，因此
縱然單一客戶或是特定世代的 AI 伺服器平台需求放緩，亦可無縫轉換產能，
較不會出現過去供過於求時，閒置產能導致的折舊與維護費用等沉沒成本，
營運風險相對較小。 
2027 年伺服器展望不變：美國期中選舉後，市場對於限制資料中心相關法
案的擔憂升溫，然而廣達表示目前客戶方面並未調整訂單。廣達的客戶以五
大 CSP 為主，Neocloud 的占比不高，就現階段來看主要 CSP 的資本支出尚
未放緩，我們預估 2027 年 AI 伺服器展望將維持不變。3Q26 AI 伺服器出貨
量的下滑主要原因為新舊平台交替的陣痛期，預估 4Q26 伺服器出貨量仍將
下滑，但因 Vera Rubin 相較 GB300 近乎倍增的 ASP，AI 伺服器營收將維持
季增。此外，目前在記憶體、PCB 及 CPU 缺貨問題對於消費性產品及通用
型伺服器影響較大，但由於廣達至 2027 年的訂單人以五大 CSP 為主，且
CSP 巨頭鎖料能力較強，因此 AI 伺服器的長短料問題相較之下較輕微，我
們預期 1Q27 Vera Rubin將可如先前預期放量，帶動 2027 年 AI 伺服器營收
高雙位數增長。通用型伺服器部分，受惠 AI Agent 的應用落地帶動通用型
伺服器需求增長，預估 4Q26 通用型伺服器將持續季增。考慮到 AI Agent 的
需求持續成長，預期 2027 年通用型伺服器除 Share gain 外，亦受惠整體市
場需求提升帶來的成長，全年營收呈雙位數年增。 
管理層預期 2027 年筆電市場持續低迷：管理層預期 2027 年筆電需求將持
續受料件漲價及缺料等問題壓抑，導致 2027 年筆電出貨量下滑高個位數至
低雙位數左右，考慮到目前筆電營收占比已不足 15%，因此我們認為 2027
年合併營收將受伺服器的強勁成長帶動而年增。 
預估 4Q26 營收 1.19 兆元，稅後 EPS 7.19 元：預期 4Q26 筆電市場仍舊低
迷，AI 伺服器處於世代交替的轉換期，出貨量將持續下滑，但受 ASP 提升
影響而帶動 AI 伺服器營收季增，此外通用型伺服器出貨亦維持強勁。綜上

4 群益投顧 
所述，群益預估 4Q26 營收 1.19 兆元，QoQ+7.0%：毛利率 4.62%， QoQ-
0.13ppts，毛利續持續下滑主要受到 AI 伺服器 ASP 提升帶來的稀釋，以及
筆電出貨量下滑造成的規模經濟效果衰減所影響；營業利益 331.68 億元，
QoQ+3.0%，營益率 2.78%；歸屬母公司淨利 288.35 億元，QoQ+1.45%；稅
後 EPS 7.02 元。 
預估 2027 年營收 5.99 兆元，稅後 EPS 30.85 元：群益預估 2027 年合併營
收 5.99 兆元，YoY+44.1%；毛利率 4.18%，YoY-0.6ppts；營業利益 1,490.08
億元，YoY+21.5%，營益率 2.49%， YoY-0.5ppts；歸屬母公司淨利 1,266.99
億元，YoY+18.3%，稅後 EPS 30.85 元。 
投資建議：廣達目前未見主要 CSP 客戶調整 AI 伺服器訂單，且考慮到 CSP
對於記憶體、CPU 和 PCB 等關鍵零組件的鎖料能力，我們預期 Vera Rubin
將於如原先預期於 1Q27 放量，帶動 2027 年 AI 伺服器營收維持高雙位數成
長。通用型伺服器則受惠於 AI Agent 帶動運算需求及市占提升，預估全年
營收呈雙位數年增。預期伺服器強勁的成長將可抵銷筆電市場的低迷，帶動
2027 年營收年增，惟 Vera Rubin 的高單價將使毛利率及營益率承壓，但營
業利益亦將仍持續增長，整體營運展望維持正向，然而考慮到近期市場對於
資料中心建置的疑慮升溫而可能抑制評價，因此我們給予廣達 Trading Buy
的投資建議，目標價 324。

  評等 定義 
  強力買進(Strong Buy)  首次評等潛在上漲空間≥ 35%  
  買進(Buy)  15% ≤首次評等潛在上漲空間<35%  
  區間操作(Trading Buy) 5%≤首次評等潛在上漲空間<15%

中立(Neutral)  
無法由基本面給予投資評等 
預期近期股價將處於盤整 
建議降低持股

【投資評等調整機制】 
  投資評等係「首次」給予特定個股投資評等時，其前一日收盤價相較 3 個月
目標價之潛在上漲空間計算而得。個股投資評等分為四個等級，定義如上。
爾後的投資評等係依循「首次評等」，直到停止推薦。  
「強力買進」、「買進」及「區間操作」均有 upside 目標價。差別僅在於，
首次評等時潛在報酬率不同。「中立」則無目標價。 
一旦我們給予特定個股「強力買進」、「買進」或「區間操作」之投資評等，
就是責任的開始，爾後將透過各式研究報告作定期性、持續性追蹤基本面及
股價變化，直到停止推薦。 
停止推薦情境： 
1. 達目標價。 
2. 雖未達原訂目標價，但檢視基本面、訊息面、籌碼面等多方訊息，研
判股價上漲空間已然有限，將適時出具降評報告。 
3. 推薦後股價不漲反跌。

【免責聲明】 
  本研究報告僅提供予特定人之客戶作為參考資料「非經同意不得轉載」。我
們並不確保此資訊的完整性與正確性，投資人應了解，報告中有關未來預測
之陳述可能不會實現，因而不應被依賴。而且此報告並非根據特定投資目的
或依預定對象之財務狀況所撰寫出來的，因此，此研究報告的目的，既非對
投資人於買賣證券、選擇權、期貨或其他證券相關之衍生性商品提供詢價服
務，亦非作為進行交易的要約。投資人應注意到相關證券之價值及收益，可
能會有無預警地上升或下降，產生投資回報金額可能比原始投資來得少的
情形。

## Document 117: 261112_daiwa_HH

### Page 1

Taiwan Information Technology

What’s new: We review Hon Hai’s 3Q26 results, announced 12 November. 
We are impressed with its opex control, which led to solid operating margin 
expansion despite gross margin dilution from rising AI server contribution.

What’s the impact: 3Q26 results beat. Net income was TWD78bn (EPS: 
TWD5.53), 25% higher than our estimate (TWD62bn) and 10% higher than 
the Bloomberg consensus (TWD70bn). Revenue was TWD3.0tn (+19% 
QoQ; +46% YoY) with better-than-expected smartphone contribution. The 
gross margin came in at 6.02% (vs. 6.12% in 2Q26), a 0.1pp decline QoQ 
on a rising cloud segment mix. That said, the operating margin expanded 
0.07pp to 3.83%, driven by strong opex control and better leverage.

Key updates: Hon Hai reaffirms its positive view of its 2027 revenue 
momentum, targeting double-digit growth YoY (similar to our forecast). The 
company believes its cloud business will remain a key growth driver (54% 
of total revenue in 3Q26). Regarding total AI server racks, Hon Hai expects 
to see high-double-digit growth in 2027 (vs. our expectation of c.30% YoY 
growth from GPU server plus a rising ASIC contribution; however, as ASIC 
AI servers typically utilise consignment models, its AI server revenue 
growth will be slightly diluted). As for smartphones, considering Hon Hai’s 
key clients’ models command high ASPs, we believe the impact from rising 
memory costs will be relatively smaller, but we expect the PC segment to 
decline YoY due to the negative impact from rising component costs.

During the 3Q26 results call, AI server end demand from the US was the 
hottest question, especially after the US mid-term elections. We believe 
management’s view on hyperscalers’ and neocloud’s near-term demand 
remains positive, but the long-term impact must be closely monitored. Also, 
as investors are worried about the AI server capex slowdown from CSPs, 
management also indicated that there would be new end demand from 
sovereign and enterprise groups, which could act as a second growth 
wave. For 4Q26, the company is guiding for >15% revenue growth QoQ 
and YoY given the ICT peak season along with rising AI server revenue (up 
by 5-15% QoQ due to ongoing AI server shipment strength).

What we recommend: We raise our 2026-28E EPS by 10-12% to reflect 
higher ASP assumptions for AI servers. We reaffirm our Buy (1) rating but 
trim the 12M TP to TWD320, using a lower target PER of 13x (prior 16x; vs. 
its past-3-year range of 8-18x) on our 1-year forward EPS, given the overall 
weaker sentiment. Downside risk: low demand for AI servers.

How we differ: Our 2026-27E revenue are above the consensus by 2-3%, 
likely as we are more optimistic on Hon Hai’s AI server shipments.

12 November 2026

3Q26 review: solid opex control

➢ OPM expansion on strong scale leverage despite GM contraction  
➢ Demand outlook remains promising even after US mid-term elections 
➢ Reiterating Buy (1) rating; trimming 12M TP to TWD320

Company profile 
Established in 1974, Hon Hai Precision Industry (Hon Hai) started out making plastic products, 
connector products and cable assemblies. It is now the largest electronics manufacturing service (EMS) 
provider globally in terms of design, manufacturing, global logistics and after-market service.

### Page 5

5

Hon Hai Precision Industry (2317 TT): 12 November 2026 
ESG analysis

Update Date: 12 Nov 2026 
Source: Daiwa, Company

ESG risks
Risks Management Analyst comments
G
Executive/board
quality 2
Hon Hai has incorporated 4 independent members into its 7-member board of directors in pursuit
of better governance. However, the company's Chairman also serves concurrently as President,
implying an absence of separation between board leadership and executive management.
Capital management 1 Hon Hai has declared >40% payout ratio for most of the years since 2014, and >50% in the past 5 years, pointing to a shareholder-friendly dividend policy.
Related party &
transaction 2
Revenue from related parties accounted for 1.6% of total revenue for 2025, while purchases
from related parties constituted 0.1% of cost of sales. We believe there is limited risk from
related-party transactions.
S Supply chain management 2 Hon Hai adheres to green design concepts and incorporates environmental factors and pollution prevention measures during the design and development stages. The company's green product design guidelines include 4 axes: "energy-saving, non-harmful, reduction and easily recyclable."
S Labor practices 2
Hon Hai recognises employees as the most important asset and continues to invest in its
workforce. In terms of safety, Hon Hai has upgraded its occupational safety and health
management system since 2019, and obtained the ISO45001 and CNS45001 certificates. The
company held online activities related to factory safety and health. Overall, we note that Hon Hai
has improved in terms of labour management in recent years. Hon Hai established a team to
implement data security management. Moreover, it plans to conduct data security training and
education regularly for all employees. Hon Hai aims to achieve zero data security incident in the
future.
S Product design & lifecycle management 2 Hon Hai adheres to green design concepts and incorporates environmental factors and pollution prevention measures during the design and development stages. The company's green product design guidelines include 4 axes: "energy-saving, non-harmful, reduction and easily recyclable."
Company specific Management Analyst comments
E GHG emissions 2
In November 2020, Hon Hai announced its commitment to achieving net zero greenhouse gas
emissions by 2050. Its Scope 1 and 2 GHG emissions decreased by 53.6% in 2025 compared
with 2020, exceeding its reduction target of 21%. That said, the company's actual energy-saving
rate in mainland China was 5.2% in 2025, successfully achieving its energy-saving goal of 4.2%.
Note: Management score represents a company's ability to manage/benefit from certain ESG topics. The scores range from 1 to 3, with 1 being the strongest.

### Page 1

Wistron

Reiterate Buy: 3Q26 beat, better margins; 
AI momentum intact; cut PO to NT$250

Reiterate Rating: BUY | PO: 250.00 TWD | Price: 174.50 TWD

3Q26 beat: solid margins on favorable product/client mix 
Wistron’s 3Q26 GM/OPM of 5.90%/4.24% came in above BofAe of 5.36%/3.66% and 
consensus of 5.55%/3.70%. We attribute the strong margins to greater sales scale 
(+28% QoQ, +102% YoY; 1%/8% above BofAe/consensus), more favorable product/client 
mix in AI business, and better results from Wiwynn, mainly supported by general/ASIC 
server momentum (see also Wiwynn 3Q26 result note). Thus, OP was 17%/24% above 
BofAe/consensus. With non-operating loss of NT$6.2bn (including net interest expense 
and FX loss), NP was 16%/27% above BofAe/consensus (Exhibit 3).  
AI order momentum intact; ongoing project ramp-ups 
We see no change in Wistron’s AI order momentum into 4Q26 and 2027-28. We expect 
4Q26/1Q27 sales to rise 11%/8% QoQ, driven by GPU/CPU rack project ramp-ups, with 
rising ASP and spec upgrade. We also see potential sales upside from more new 
client/project wins in 2H27-2028. Although the GM decline trend is inevitable for AI 
server ODMs, we expect Wistron to post solid core OPM (i.e. excluding Wiwynn) into 
2027-28, with rising operating efficiency and more diversified client/project base. On 10 
Nov, the company announced additional capex plans of subtotal around NT$12.8bn for 
adding facilities and equipment in Taiwan, mainly to support new AI-related businesses, 
and another US$17.8mn for expanding server manufacturing capacity in Mexico.  
Trim PO to NT$250 with a lower P/E set-up; reiterate Buy  
We lift our 2026-28E EPS by 7-21% (but 2027-28E EPS is still 8-9% below consensus) 
to reflect the 3Q26 results beat, solid AI momentum, and sustained core OPM. Yet, we 
lower our PO to NT$250 (12x 4Q26-3Q27E EPS) from NT$265 (15x 2H26-1H27E EPS), 
with the valuation base rollover and a lower P/E multiple. We now apply 12x (1SD above 
historical average since 2022; Exhibit 8), vs. prior 15x (1.5SD above) on valuation de-
rating across AI supply chain amid macro uncertainty. Nevertheless, we reiterate Buy on 
Wistron in view of its solid AI sales momentum with a broadening client/project base.

Key Metrics      
* For full definitions of iQmethod SM measures, see page 9.

Company Sector 
IT Hardware

Company Description 
Wistron is one of the top-5 ODMs globally. Server/storage 
and PC business accounted for 73% and 24% of sales in 
2025, respectively. Key server/storage customers include 
Dell, Lenovo, HPE, Cisco, Nvidia, AMD, Intel. Key NB 
customers include Dell, Lenovo, Acer. Wistron was spun off 
from Acer (2353 TT) in 2000 and was incorporated in May 
2001. It went publicly listed on the Taiwan stock exchange 
in Aug 2003. It is headquartered in Taipei, Taiwan.

Investment Rationale 
We assign a Buy rating to Wistron in view of improving 
sales/profit mix, solid outlook from Wiwynn, high exposure 
to server business after exiting from low-margin iPhone 
assembly business, and improving core OPM. We believe 
Wistron will benefit from Dell's strong demand in Nvidia AI 
GPU/CPU rack as the key supplier.

### Page 7

Price objective basis & risk 
Wistron (WICOF) 
Our PO of NT$250 is based on 12x 4Q26-3Q27E EPS. We use 12x valuation multiple 
(around 1SD above historical P/E average since 2022) in view of high server mix, a key 
position in AI server supply chain with exposure both to component/rack business, and 
ongoing core OPM improvement.

Upside risks to our PO are (1) stronger server market demand, especially for AI, (2) 
stronger PC demand, (3) share/order win in AI server business, and (4) favorable FX 
trend.

Downside risks to our PO are (1) softer AI server demand due to AI GPU short supply or 
supply chain hiccup, (2) weaker PC market demand amid inflation/macro uncertainties, 
(3) GM pressure due to higher labor/logistics cost, (4) more intensified competition in 
NB and server business, and (5) unfavorable FX movement (USD/RMB and/or USD/NTD) 
to weigh on GM and/or non-operating items.

iQmethod SMis the set of BofA Global Research standard measures that serve to maintain global consistency under three broad headings: Business Performance, Quality of Earnings, and validations. The key features of 
iQmethod are: A consistently structured, detailed, and transparent methodology. Guidelines to maximize the effectiveness of the comparative valuation process, and to identify some common pitfalls. 
iQdatabase ®  is our real-time global research database that is sourced directly from our equity analysts’ earnings models and includes forecasted as well as historical data for income statements, balance sheets, and cash 
flow statements for companies covered by BofA Global Research.

of potential price fluctuation, are: A - Low, B - Medium and C - High. INVESTMENT RATINGS reflect the analyst’s assessment of both a stock’s absolute total return 
potential as well as its attractiveness for investment relative to other stocks within its Coverage Cluster (defined below). Our investment ratings are: 1 - Buy stocks 
are expected to have a total return of at least 10% and are the most attractive stocks in the coverage cluster; 2 - Neutral stocks are expected to remain flat or 
increase in value and are less attractive than Buy rated stocks and 3 - Underperform stocks are the least attractive stocks in a coverage cluster. An investment 
rating of 6 (No Rating) indicates that a stock is no longer trading on the basis of fundamentals. Analysts assign investment ratings considering, among other things, 
the 0-12 month total return expectation for a stock and the firm’s guidelines for ratings dispersions (shown in the table below). The current price objective for a 
stock should be referenced to better understand the total return expectation at any given time. The price objective reflects the analyst’s view of the potential price 
appreciation (depreciation).

Update
Co-Tech Development Corp.  | Asia Pacific
Key Japanese Competitor
Announced Price Hikes
What's new? Our supply chain checks suggest that one of Co-Tech's key Japanese
competitors notified customers earlier this week of price increases for its
electrodeposited (ED) copper foil products. The company will raise prices by 30%
for ¼ oz (9µm) and ⅓ oz (12µm) ED copper foils and by 15% for other thicknesses,
effective for shipments from December 2026 onward. This aligns with our previous

supply chain check findings published in our Nov 4 report (link).
Our view: The increases extend beyond high-end HVLP products to copper foils
used in consumer electronics. We believe this reflects rising production costs and,
more importantly, tightening supply, with positive implications for Co-Tech. We
expect Co-Tech to follow with further price increases, although likely at smaller
magnitudes given the ~10% hike it implemented in October.
Morgan Stanley Taiwan Limited+

Key Takeaways
Earlier this week, a key Japanese competitor issued a price increase notice to its
customers for electrodeposited copper foils.
This aligns with our  supply chain check findings published in our Nov 4 report.
We believe the  price hikes have positive implications for Co-Tech and expect Co-
Tech to follow with price increases as well.

However, since Co-Tech already implemented a ~10% price hike in October, we
don't expect it to announce a price hike of the same magnitude.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.

研 究 報 告                
穩懋(3105.TT) 
產業：電子–半導體 
2027 光通訊跟衛星本業雙引擎 認列聯茂收益業外動能強勁 
 投資評價與建議 
公司過往Forward PER落在20~30倍，考量光通訊與衛星業務出貨放量，毛利
率迎來結構性跳升，有利估值重估，維持「增加持股」評等。 
 重點評論分析 
3Q26營收符合預期，毛利率因產品組合優化上升至37.4%：3Q26營收58.6億
元，季增11%，營收符合預期主要係因傳統消費性電子旺季，加上航太衛星與
光電產品動能強勁，毛利率37.4%，季增9.2個百分點，主要係因產品組合優化，
航太衛星與光電產品營收占比過半，加上產能利用率上升至75%，業外收入
6.98億元，主因聯茂貢獻業外EPS約1.61元，稅後EPS 4.55元。 
4Q26營運淡季不淡，2027成⻑動能聚焦Optical跟Infrastructure：(1)4Q26
面臨手機傳統淡季與年底例行性歲修，但受惠於PD產能持續開出與其他產品
線營收持平至微幅成⻑，預估4Q26營收將季增1~3%，毛利率37~39%。(2)光
通訊PD於3Q26逐步放量，4Q26將持續成⻑，預估2027年大幅放量，CW Laser
正進行可靠度測試，預估2027年小量貢獻，顯著營收爆發期落在2028年，EML
處於產品開發與測試階段，2028年才有機會產生營收貢獻。(3)航太衛星帶動
Infrastructure成為3Q26季增幅度最高的產品線，衛星佔Infrastructure營收
比重已升至40%，超越傳統基地台。(4)預估聯茂2027年EPS 72.22元，穩懋採
權益法認列持股23%之投資收益約61.7億元，貢獻EPS約14.56元。 
 投資風險 
光通PD跟雷射放量不如預期、衛星發射遞延。

### Page 2

2 
| 營運概況 
3Q26 產品組合優化帶動毛利率與 EPS 創高：3Q26 營收 58.6 億元，季增 11%，營收符合預期主要係因傳統消費
性電子旺季，加上航太衛星與光電產品動能強勁，毛利率 37.4%，季增 9.2 個百分點，優於財測，主要係因產品
組合優化，航太衛星與光電產品營收占比過半，加上產能利用率上升至 75%，營業利益率 24.2%，業外收入 6.98
億元，因持有聯茂 23%股權並採權益法認列投資收益，聯茂貢獻業外 EPS 約 1.61 元，稅後 EPS 4.55 元。 
4Q26 營運淡季不淡，2027 成⻑動能聚焦 Optical 跟 Infrastructure：(1)儘管 4Q26 面臨手機傳統淡季與年底例
行性歲修，但受惠於 PD 產能持續開出與其他產品線營收持平至微幅成⻑，預估 4Q26 營收將季增 1~3%，毛利
率 37~39%，主要受惠產品組合優化跟產能利用率維持 70%以上。(2)中階手機受臨中國 PA 供應鏈本土化及記憶
體缺貨的逆風，但穩懋聚焦中高階手機市場，動能維持穩健，4Q26 Cellular 營收僅呈現小幅下滑，WiFi 產品在
Router 需求回流下，3Q26 雙位數成⻑，4Q26 呈現持平至微幅成⻑。(3)光通訊 PD 於 3Q26 逐步放量，4Q26
將持續成⻑，預估 2027 年產能完全開出並大幅放量，CW Laser 正進行可靠度測試，預估 2027 年小量貢獻，顯
著營收爆發期落在 2028，EML 處於產品開發與測試階段，2028 年才有機會產生營收貢獻。(4)航太衛星帶動
Infrastructure 成為 3Q26 季增幅度最高的產品線，衛星佔 Infrastructure 營收比重已升至 40%，超越傳統基地
台，目前 GaN 月產能是 300 片左右，公司積極擴充 GaN 產能，預計 1Q27 導入 GaN 6 吋製程生產。(5)聯茂
2027 年爆發性成⻑，預估聯茂 2027 年 EPS 72.22 元，穩懋採權益法認列持股 23%之投資收益約 61.7 億元，貢
獻EPS約14.56元。

| 公 司 簡 介 
穩懋(3105)為全球最大的化合物半導體晶圓代工廠，核心業務聚焦於微波通訊與光電元件的砷化鎵(GaAs)及氮化
鎵(GaN)晶圓代工服務，公司總部及研發中心設於台灣桃園龜山(華亞科技園區)，主要六吋晶圓生產基地位於桃
園華亞廠及龜山廠，總產能約 4.3 萬片，3Q26 產能利用率為 75%。3Q26 年營收比重為 Cellular(手機
PA)25~30%、Infrastructure(通訊基礎建設)35~40%、Wi-Fi 15~20%、Optical 10~15%、其他 3%。主要客
戶包含 Broadcom、Lumentum、Murata 等國際大廠，銷售地區為台灣 8%、亞洲 70%、美洲 15%、歐洲
7%。 
穩懋的微波通訊產品處理高頻訊號的放大與切換，主要元件為異質接面雙載子電晶體(HBT)與假晶高電子遷移率
電晶體(pHEMT) ，用於智慧型手機功率放大器(PA)、Wi-Fi 路由器及低軌衛星設備的射頻(RF)訊號，提供低雜

AI 高階測試需求維持強勁，成長趨勢不變 
◼ 產品組合有望優化，預估 4Q26 稅後 EPS 21.18 元：CPC 及 VPC
稼動率皆維持高檔，預估 4Q26 營收 63.95 億元(+66.7%YoY)。獲利
方面，受惠產品組合持續優化，預估毛利率季增至 61.92%，隨營收
規模持續擴大，費用率有望年減至 25%，營益率 37.12%，稅後淨
利 20.75 億元(+119%YoY)，預估 4Q26 稅後 EPS 21.18 元。 
◼ 產能擴充計畫持續推進，預估 2026 年稅後 EPS 68.44 元：展望後
市，隨 AI HPC 需求持續成長，探針卡朝高頻高速、大電流及高針腳
數方向發展，帶動高階測試需求維持強勁。為解決產能瓶頸，旺矽
將積極擴充產能，2026/2027 年底 MEMS 月產能為 350 萬/600 萬
針，VPC 月產能為 200 萬/250 萬針，且 PCB 廠房預計 2H27 投產，
有助提升 PCB 自製率。整體而言，預估 2026/2027 年稅後 EPS 
68.44/118.84 元，且 CPO 相關設備有望於 2028 年較明顯挹注營
收，預估 2028 年稅後 EPS 204.56 元。 
◼ 維持買進評等，目標價 6,500 元：評價方面，以 2028 年 EPS 估算，
目前 PE 約為 21X，低於旺矽近 2 年 30-55X，考量 AI 高階測試需求
持續強勁，且 CPO 布局有望挹注中長期成長動能，長線維持買進評
等，目標價調整為 6,500 元(2028F PE 32X)。

旺矽為全球前五大探針卡廠商  旺矽 1995 年 7 月於新竹成立，實收資本額 9.8 億元，截至 Apr-26，員工人數
共 2,110 人。主要業務包含晶圓探針卡測試及半導體設備銷售。旺矽提供一站
式購足服務，探針卡業務包含懸臂式探針卡(Cantilever Probe Cards)、垂直式
探針卡(Vertical Probe Cards)及微機電探針卡(MEMS)等；半導體設備則例如
光電自動化設備(PA)、溫控測試設備(Thermal)、先進半導體測試設備
(Advanced Semiconductor Test, AST)等。以 3Q26 產品別營收結構而言，探
針卡 72.7%，設備 25.4%，其他 1.9%。

產品組合有望優化，預估 4Q26
稅後 EPS 21.18 元 
 由於 AI HPC 客 戶 拉 貨 動 能 持 續 強 勁 ， 旺 矽 Oct-26 營收 22.32 億元
(+8.98%MoM，+92.73%YoY)，優於預期。考量 CPC 及 VPC 稼動率皆維持高
檔，預估 4Q26 營收 63.95 億元(+8.3%QoQ，+66.7%YoY)。獲利方面，受惠
產品組合持續優化，預估毛利率季增至 61.92%，隨營收規模持續擴大，費用
率有望年減至 25%，營益率 37.12%，稅後淨利 20.75 億元(+10.4%QoQ，
+119%YoY)，預估 4Q26 稅後 EPS 21.18 元。

產能擴充計畫持續推進，預 估
2026 年稅後 EPS 68.44 元

展望後市，隨 AI 與 HPC 需求持續成長，探針卡朝高頻高速、大電流及高針腳
數方向發展，帶動高階測試需求維持強勁。為解決產能瓶頸，旺矽將積極擴充
產能，包含：MEMS 月產能自 2025 年底 100 萬針，預期 2026 年底提升至 350
萬針，2027 年底目標為 600 萬針；VPC 月產能則自 2025 年底 120 萬針，預
期 2026 年底提升為 200 萬針，2027 年底有望再增加至 250 萬針；CPC 月產
能則維持持平略增。廠房方面，旺矽 Nov-26 中旬公告投入 22 億元進行湖口廠
機電工程，PCB 廠房預計如期於 2H27 投產，有助提升 PCB 自製率；另，
CPO 設備所需廠房改建，預計 1Q27 完成。資本支出方面，預估 2026/2027 年
分別為 15-18 億元及 20 億元，2027 年主要用於設備採購。

整體而言，投顧預估 2026 年營收 214.7 億元(+60.5%YoY)，受惠產品組合持
續優化，毛利率 60.5%，營益率 36%，稅後淨利 67.1 億元(+111.1%YoY)，上
修 2026 年稅後 EPS 至 68.44 元(原預估 64.97 元)。

Exhibit 6：旺矽擴產時程推估

Sources: Company，Taishin 
高階測試需求續強，預估 2027
年稅後 EPS 118.84 元 
 旺矽自 2026 年起整併 LED 與 AST 部門，並持續布局 CPO 檢測專案，目前主
要聚焦於 CPO insertion 2 之 EIC/PIC 測試及 insertion 3 之 die-level 測試應
用。由於 CPO 測試涉及光電整合，技術門檻及客製化程度較高，目前仍持續
與客戶進行驗證及開發。其中，insertion 2 既有客戶預計 2026 年底更新驗證
進度，另有新客戶持續接洽中，相關進展有望於 1H27 較為明朗；insertion 3
則已有多家國際客戶出貨工程機，且持續交機中，有望 1H27 逐步轉為量產機
需求。展望後市，隨 AI 高速傳輸需求提升，CPO 相關測試設備有望成為 AST
業務中長期成長動能，惟考量設備交期較長及客戶驗收認證所需時間，預期
2028 年營收貢獻將較為顯著，CPO 相關業務營收占比有望達雙位數。

展望 2027 年，隨新增產能逐步開出及新廠投入營運，且客戶組成較為多元，

## Document 248: [329180] HD현대중공업(329180) 피크아웃 우려를 이겨낼 조선사HD현대중공업(329180) 피크아웃 우려를 이겨낼 조선사피크아웃 우려를 이겨낼 조선사 - 기업

Source PDF: all report pdf/[329180] HD현대중공업(329180) 피크아웃 우려를 이겨낼 조선사HD현대중공업(329180) 피크아웃 우려를 이겨낼 조선사피크아웃 우려를 이겨낼 조선사 - 기업.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-10-29
Pages: 4
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

(329180) 
HD 현대중공업 
2026.10.29

AI 로보틱스/방산/조선/운송 양승윤 
6139 / syyang0901@eugenefn.com

피크아웃 우려를 이겨낼 조선사 
 투자의견 BUY(유지) 
 목표주가 610,000 원(하향) 
 현재주가 285,500 원(10/28)

 3Q26 매출액은 5조 7,536억원(+30.2%yoy), 영업이익은 8,992억원
(+61.3%yoy, OPM 15.6%)을 기록. 영업이익 컨센서스 9,792억원 하회.  
 평균 환율이 약 79원 하락하며 영업이익 약 450억원이 줄었고, 조업
일수는 47일로 통상 계절성 대비 4일 추가 감소해 약 500억원의 이
익 감소 효과 발생. 임단협 추가 비용의 약 75%도 3분기에 선반영
(잔여분은 4분기 반영). 일회성 요인을 제외하면 이익 체력은 견조. 
 동사의 조선 부문 매출액은 4조 3,988억원(+44.0%yoy), 영업이익은 
6,463억원(+62.8%yoy, OPM 14.7%)을 기록. 상선 OPM은 14.7%로 
2Q26(17.8%) 대비 하락했으나 대부분 외생변수 영향. 3Q26 대형선 매출 
중 2023년 수주분 비중은 45%, 2024~2025년 수주분은 51%. 고선가 
물량의 매출 비중이 높아지는 2027년까지 수익성 개선 여력은 충분.  
 9월 말 누적 상선 수주는 138.0억달러로 연간 목표의 120%, 엔진기계는 
41.4억달러로 155%를 달성했으나 함정(13%)과 해양(5%)은 부진. LNG선
은 중국 조선소의 저가 수주와 발주 둔화로 선가가 일부 하락. 선주들이 현 
선가를 저점으로 인식하며 발주 협의를 재개하는 분위기. 북미 LNG 프로젝
트 FID가 이어지고 있어 2027년 LNG선 발주 회복 기대. 
 함정 부문은 매출 2,570억원(-14.6%qoq)으로 줄었으나, 수출 비중 확대에 
따른 믹스 개선으로 영업이익 386억원(+114%qoq, OPM 15.0%)을 기록. 
해양플랜트는 매출액 3,799억원(+35.3%yoy), 영업이익 1,265억원(OPM 
33.3%). C/O 180억원을 제외해도 Ruya 공정 본격화와 Trion 고수익 물량
으로 높은 수익성 유지. 2027년 일부 프로젝트 인도로 매출 감소가 예상되
나, LNG선·블록 물량 투입으로 고정비 부담을 완화할 계획. 신규 해상변전
소(OSS) 계약을 앞두고 있으며, 2027년 초 추가 수주 발표도 기대. 
 주목할 부분은 엔진기계. 영업이익 2,299억원(OPM 24.2%)으로 높은 D/F 
엔진 비중을 바탕으로 견조한 수익성 유지. 육상발전용 힘센엔진은 올해   
3GW를 확보하며 2029년까지 계획 캐파 수준의 물량을 이미 채움. 생산 
예상 물량은 2028년 약 1.5GW, 2029년 약 3GW이며, 신규 공장 구축 이
후 연간 생산능력은 약 4GW로 확대 예정. 북미 Hyperscaler와의 직접 협
상이 늘고 있으며, 힘센엔진을 활용한 부유식 발전설비(BMPP)로 FDC 시장
까지 대응 가능. AI 데이터센터 전력 수요가 2028년 이후 새로운 이익 축. 
 미국에서는 Kiewit과 합작법인 설립 계약 체결. 군수지원함 등 지원함 사업
부터 추진하며, 현지 조선소 건설 과정에서 HII에 선박 블록을 공급하는 방
안도 협의 중. 구체적인 투자계획은 2026년 말까지 확정 목표. 국내에서는 
핵추진잠수함 초기 예산이 2027년 반영되며 동사도 사업 참여를 준비 중. 
 동사에 대한 투자의견 BUY, 목표주가 61만원을 제시. 고선가 물량 매출 확
대와 육상발전용 엔진 성장으로 피크아웃을 이겨낼 굴지의 조선사.

Compliance Notice 
당사는 자료 작성일 기준으로 지난 3개월 간 해당종목에 대해서 유가증권 발행에 참여한 적이 없습니다 
당사는 본 자료 발간일을 기준으로 해당종목의 주식을 1% 이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다  
당사는 동 자료를 기관투자가 또는 제 3자에게 사전 제공한 사실이 없습니다 
조사분석담당자는 자료작성일 현재 동 종목과 관련하여 재산적 이해관계가 없습니다 
동 자료에 게재된 내용들은 조사분석담당자 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하고 있으며, 외부의 부당한 압력이나 간섭 없이 작성되었음을 확인합니다 
동 자료는 당사의 제작물로서 모든 저작권은 당사에게 있습니다 
동 자료는 당사의 동의 없이 어떠한 경우에도 어떠한 형태로든 복제, 배포, 전송, 변형, 대여할 수 없습니다 
동 자료에 수록된 내용은 당사 리서치센터가 신뢰할 만한 자료 및 정보로부터 얻어진 것이나, 당사는 그 정확성이나 완전성을 보장할 수 없습니다. 따라서 어떠
한 경우에도 자료는 고객의 주식투자의 결과에 대한 법적 책임소재에 대한 증빙자료로 사용될 수 없습니다

### Page 2

2

Điểm nhấn thị trường 
Chỉ số, nhóm ngành, khối ngoại và phái sinh

Top 5 ngành tăng / giảm  VNINDEX tăng điểm trong tuần qua, +68.24 điểm tương ứng +4.00%, với thanh khoản cải 
thiện. Thiết bị điện, Khách sạn - Nhà hàng và Thiết bị dịch vụ y tế là các nhóm ngành có 
mức tăng rõ rệt nhất trong tuần. Khối ngoại duy trì bán ròng so với xu hướng của tuần 
trước với giá trị -966 tỷ đồng, tập trung ở các mã FPT, ACB và VCB; mặt khác, tự doanh duy 
trì mua ròng ở mức 137 tỷ đồng.

Ở một khía cạnh khác, khối ngoại đảo chiều mua ròng 1,335 HĐTL VN30F1M trong tuần 
qua nhưng đang duy trì vị thế bán lũy kế -3,049 hợp đồng. Tự doanh đảo chiều mua ròng 
1,815 hợp đồng nhưng đang duy trì trạng thái lũy kế bán ròng -6,000 hợp đồng.

TTCK Việt Nam hồi phục trở lại sau bốn tuần giảm liên tiếp, khi VN-Index bật tăng mạnh 
từ vùng hỗ trợ quanh 1.700 điểm với thanh khoản cải thiện. Sau nhịp điều chỉnh kéo dài 
khiến mặt bằng định giá về vùng hấp dẫn, lực cầu bắt đáy đã nhập cuộc quyết liệt hơn, 
với phiên 21/10 tăng gần 47 điểm — một trong những phiên tăng mạnh nhất nhiều tháng 
gần đây. Điểm tích cực và đáng chú ý nhất là khối ngoại đã xuất hiện hai phiên đảo chiều 
mua ròng trở lại sau khoảng 20 phiên bán ròng liên tiếp, tập trung vào nhóm Bluechip 
như VIC, TCB, VHM, MSN — tín hiệu đầu tiên cho thấy áp lực rút vốn ngoại có thể đang dần 
hạ nhiệt. Thông tin tích cực tác động lên thị trường đến từ các chỉ đạo của NHNN và Chính 
phủ về việc hạ mặt bằng lãi suất để hỗ trợ tăng trưởng kinh tế. Bên cạnh đó, mùa báo cáo 
KQKD quý 3 khả quan đang diễn ra cũng là yếu tố hỗ trợ xu hướng thị trường chung.

Nguồn: V_KBSource

Triển vọng kinh tế 4Q2026 
Giữ nhịp tăng trưởng, vững vàng về itch

Một số dự báo chính của KBSV về kinh tế Việt Nam trong năm 2026:

1) Tăng trưởng GDP năm 2026 được dự báo sẽ đạt 9.0%, với động lực chính 
đến từ định hướng đẩy mạnh giải ngân đầu tư công của Chính phủ, dòng 
vốn FDI duy trì tích cực và hoạt động sản xuất công nghiệp phục hồi theo 
xuất khẩu. Về rủi ro, chúng tôi cho rằng Việt Nam sẽ tiếp tục đối mặt với 
một số yếu tố bất định từ bên ngoài, bao gồm: rủi ro xung đột Mỹ – Iran 
kéo dài hơn dự kiến, xu hướng tăng của mặt bằng lãi suất toàn cầu gây gia 
tăng áp lực tài chính và kéo giảm tổng cầu, cùng nguy cơ vỡ bong bóng AI.

2) Chúng tôi nâng dự báo lạm phát bình quân năm 2026 của Việt Nam lên 
mức 4.7% YoY, cao hơn mục tiêu kiểm soát lạm phát của Chính phủ, dựa 
trên kịch bản dự báo thận trọng hơn về diễn biến xung đột địa chính trị và 
giá năng lượng thế giới trong ngắn hạn.

3) Mặt bằng lãi suất trong quý IV được dự báo sẽ ổn định quanh vùng hiện 
tại và không xuất hiện thêm nhịp tăng đột biến, nhờ dòng tiền cải thiện 
từ giải ngân đầu tư công và kỳ vọng có thêm các biện pháp hỗ trợ thanh 
khoản trung dài hạn từ phía Chính phủ và NHNN.

4) Tỷ giá USD/VND trong quý cuối năm có thể chịu một số sức ép do Fed dự 
báo còn thêm một lần tăng lãi suất nữa trong năm. Dù vậy, chúng tôi cho 
rằng tỷ giá USD/VND khó tăng mạnh mà chỉ quay lại mức đầu năm, dựa 
trên kỳ vọng chỉ số DXY không biến động mạnh, nguồn cung ngoại tệ cải 
thiện (FDI giải ngân, kiều hối, nâng hạng TTCK) và chênh lệch lãi suất 
VND–USD duy trì trạng thái dương.

Tham khảo nội dung chi tiết trong báo cáo tại đây.

21/10/2026

CTCP VÀNG BẠC ĐÁ QUÝ PHÚ NHUẬN (PNJ)  
Cập nhật ĐHĐCĐ bất thường 21/10/2026

Tái cơ cấu để xử lý áp lực thanh khoản  
— ĐHĐCĐ bất thường PNJ ngày 21/10/2026 đã thông qua điều chỉnh kế hoạch kinh 
doanh và phương án phát hành riêng lẻ để bổ sung vốn tái cấu trúc. Ban lãnh đạo đặt 
ra các vấn đề trọng yếu là đáp ứng nhu cầu thanh khoản và chuẩn bị nguồn lực để thực 
hiện các cam kết thu đổi đã tích lũy trong quá khứ. Bên cạnh đó, các cổ đông tham dự 
cũng đặt các câu hỏi liên quan đến tình hình hoạt động qua các điểm chính (1) dòng 
tiền hoạt động kinh doanh; (2) diễn biến thị trường sau khi PNJ áp dụng chính sách thu 
đổi mới; (3) hiệu quả hoạt động sau tái cấu trúc.

Nội dung chính tại đại hội 
— Theo Ban lãnh đạo, thông tin liên quan đến P-Lab từ đầu tháng 7 khiến khách hàng gia 
tăng bán lại sản phẩm. Tổng giá trị thu đổi và mua lại trong tháng đạt gần 6,000 tỷ VND, 
so với khoảng 7,000 tỷ VND trong cả năm 2025. Theo BCTC riêng, nguồn tiền khả dụng 
giảm từ khoảng 3,700 tỷ VND cuối Q2/2026 xuống 770 tỷ VND cuối Q3/2026, đã bao 
gồm khoản vay 700 tỷ VND từ Chủ tịch HĐQT. Tỷ trọng kim cương và trang sức kim 
cương trong tổng tồn kho tăng từ 41% lên 52%. 
— Q3/2026 ghi nhận lỗ sau thuế 258 tỷ VND trước phần tăng trích lập dự phòng. Để giảm 
áp lực thanh toán, PNJ kéo dài thời gian chi trả đối với kim cương lên 120 ngày từ tháng 
07/2026. Thay đổi chính sách cũng ảnh hưởng đến niềm tin khách hàng và sức mua, cụ 
thể doanh thu và lợi nhuận gộp Q3/2026 đạt lần lượt 6,995/609 tỷ VND, giảm 14%/62% 
YoY. Chi phí bán hàng và quản lý giảm 13% YoY chưa đủ bù đắp mức giảm lợi nhuận 
gộp, khiến PNJ lỗ sau thuế 258 tỷ VND, so với lãi 496 tỷ VND trong Q3/2025. 
— PNJ nâng giả định tỷ lệ thu đổi trên giá trị thu đổi còn lại lên 80%, so với 28.8% đối với 
nhóm sản phẩm giai đoạn 2020–2026 trong ước tính tại BCTC bán niên 2026. Tổng dự 
phòng tổn thất dự kiến tăng từ 2,267 tỷ VND lên 7,071 tỷ VND. Kế hoạch điều chỉnh ghi 
nhận doanh thu 39,057 tỷ VND, lỗ trước thuế 6,059 tỷ VND và lỗ sau thuế 6,271 tỷ VND. 
Theo Ban lãnh đạo, LNST loại trừ dự phòng ước khoảng 800 tỷ VND, chủ yếu nhờ kết 
quả Q1/2026. Khoản dự phòng phản ánh tổn thất ước tính cho toàn bộ kịch bản, không 
đồng nghĩa toàn bộ giá trị sẽ được chi trả ngay trong kỳ. 
— Giá trị cam kết thu đổi tích lũy đối với kim cương và trang sức kim cương tại cuối 
Q3/2026 được PNJ ước tính khoảng 21,000 tỷ VND. Với hàng đã bán, doanh nghiệp dự 
kiến chuyển đổi quyền lợi khách hàng sang sản phẩm và các chương trình ưu đãi. Với 
sản phẩm bán mới, chính sách thu đổi định hướng theo giá thị trường tại thời điểm 
giao dịch, thay cho tỷ lệ cố định trên giá trị hóa đơn, nhằm hạn chế tiếp tục tích lũy 
nghĩa vụ tiềm tàng trong tương lai theo cơ chế cũ. 
Tham khảo nội dung chi tiết trong báo cáo tại đây.

Ngưỡng kỹ thuật

VNINDEX hình thành cây nến xanh thân dài trên khung tuần và đáng chú ý là cho phản ứng 
bật tăng ngay tại cạnh dưới của mẫu hình tam giác đi ngang trung hạn, cho thấy tín hiệu 
tích cực đã quay trở lại với xu hướng ngắn hạn. Trên khung ngày, sau 2 phiên điều chỉnh, 
rung lắc vào đầu tuần, chỉ số cũng đảo chiều tăng điểm khá dốc, với 3 cây nến xanh liên 
tiếp với thanh khoản được tăng cường, củng cố thêm tín hiệu xác nhận cho đà hồi phục. 
Sau nhịp bật tăng mạnh từ đáy, VNINDEX được dự báo sẽ sớm gặp phải áp lực điều chỉnh 
khi quay lên điểm giữa của dải đi ngang theo mẫu hình tam giác, tương ứng vùng kháng cự 
kế tiếp tại quanh 1810. Tuy nhiên, xu hướng ngắn hạn vẫn có phần nghiêng về chiều tăng, 
với tín hiệu xác nhận đáy của một số mã cổ phiếu sau quá trình sụt giảm kéo dài.

(Ghi chú: NĐT có thể dựa theo nhận định xu hướng thị trường để tham khảo khuyến nghị 
hành động tương ứng trong bảng phía trên)

- Lợi nhuận kỳ vọng được tính theo giá khuyến nghị mua.

## Document 329: Global Economics Analyst_ Macro Outlook 2027_ Steady Baseline, Downside Risks

Source PDF: all report pdf/Global Economics Analyst_ Macro Outlook 2027_ Steady Baseline, Downside Risks.pdf
Report source: Goldman Sachs Publishing
Date: 2026-11-12
Pages: 30
Extraction status: ok

### Page 2

Macro Outlook 2027: Steady Baseline, Downside Risks

We entered 2026 expecting sturdy global growth and slower inflation. The war in Iran 
derailed this inflation forecast, but the global economy proved more resilient than 
feared at the start of the war. 
Even though global headline inflation is on course to end 2026 at 3.7% on a Q4/Q4 
basis—1.3pp higher than our forecast from last year—we estimate that the global 
economy still grew at a remarkably unremarkable 2.7%, in line with potential and only 
0.1pp below our projections from our 2026 outlook (Exhibit 1). Our interpretation is that 
surging AI capex, easier financial conditions, and (in the US, Germany, and parts of Asia) 
fiscal expansion mostly offset the energy supply shock. As a result of these tailwinds, 
GDP growth only modestly underperformed in the US (-0.3pp), Euro area (-0.3pp), and 
China (-0.2pp) while significantly outperforming in Asian economies such as Taiwan 
(+8.6pp) and Korea (+1.9pp) that benefited from surging AI exports.

Growth Remains Solid but Unremarkable in 2027 
We expect another year of solid but unremarkable GDP growth in 2027, but several of 
the impulses that shaped global growth in 2026 will look different. 
The headwind from the energy supply shock will likely flip next year as energy prices 
normalize. Our commodity strategists’ baseline is that continued Middle East supply 
adaptation will lower Brent prices to $78/bbl by end-2027 with a similar normalization 
in global LNG prices (Exhibit 2). Based on these projections, we forecast that the energy 
price impulse to global growth will swing from a 0.4pp drag on global growth in 2026 to 
a modest boost in 2027 (although we see a continued drag in Europe from delayed 
energy cost pass-through to consumers).

Exhibit 1: Inflation Surged, but Growth Proved Resilient in 2026

### Page 3

A positive growth impulse from energy prices should outweigh the effect of tighter 
financial conditions and a smaller fiscal impulse in 2027. Our GS Financial Conditions 
Index (GS FCI) has tightened by around 50bp in the US and by 30bp in developed 
markets (DMs) over the last three months on the back of lower equity prices and higher 
rates. It now implies a moderate drag on growth over the next few quarters (Exhibit 3). 
We do not expect meaningful fiscal consolidation owing to higher rates in 2027, but the 
large fiscal expansions of the last few years are fading. The main exception is Japan, 
where consumption tax cuts, an increase in defense spending, and the Takaichi 
administration’s investment plans point to a meaningful 0.7pp boost to GDP growth. We 
also expect modestly positive impulses in Germany and the US—where the Democratic 
victory in November’s US election suggests some of the spending cuts from the 2025 
One Big Beautiful Bill Act will be reversed—but a continued fiscal drag in the UK that 
leaves the DM fiscal impulse only slightly positive.

The main global growth impulse that we do not expect to change in 2027 is increased AI 
capex. We forecast that nominal global AI investment will rise from $1.1tn in 2026 to 
$1.8tn in 2027, mostly driven by an increase of over $500bn in hardware spending in the 
US (Exhibit 4). We expect the investment surge to be frontloaded, with the 
year-over-year change in investment as a share of GDP peaking in 2027H1 (the level of 
investment as a share of GDP peaks in 2028 under our forecast). Even with the expected 
slowdown, the impact on GDP growth will be sizable: we estimate that AI investment will 
boost GDP growth by 0.4pp globally. And although we again estimate only a small 0.2pp 
boost to measured US GDP growth in 2027 owing to continued undercounting of 
semiconductor investment and chip-related intellectual property exports, we estimate a 
larger 0.6pp boost to “true” GDP growth after correcting for these measurement biases.

The other notable growth impulse from 2026 that will continue in 2027 is a drag on 
rest-of-the-world growth from China. Our forecasts for China remain above consensus 
for 2027 (4.5% GS vs. 4.4% consensus), but this again reflects very robust expectations 
for Chinese exports and a further increase in China’s trade surplus (Exhibit 5). By 
contrast, we expect domestic demand growth to remain weak in 2027 owing to a 1.3pp 
of GDP property sector drag and weak underlying consumer spending, resulting in a 
modest 0.1pp headwind to GDP growth for the rest of the world, with larger growth 
drags in manufacturing-heavy economies such as Germany (-0.3pp) and Central Europe 
(-0.2pp).

### Page 6

Taken together, our 2027 global GDP growth forecast is modestly optimistic at 2.8%, a 
pace that is 0.1pp above our estimate of potential. As has been the case for the last 
several years, we are more optimistic on US growth (2.4% vs. 2.2% consensus) owing to 
resilient investment and more downbeat on the European growth outlook (0.9% vs. 
1.3%) owing to tighter financial conditions and our expectation that higher energy 
prices will start to bite this winter. We also expect continued robust growth in South 
Korea (3.2% vs. 2.7%) and Taiwan (6.3% vs. 5.9%) reflecting another year of strong AI 
exports.

Downside Risks 
We see risks around our baseline global growth forecasts as skewed to the downside for 
three reasons. 
1. A Renewed Energy Shock 
The first and most immediate risk is that an escalation of the war in Iran could lead to 
more significant energy price increases, creating growth headwinds that—unlike in 
2026—FCI and fiscal impulses would not offset in 2027. While our commodity strategists 
see risks around their energy price baselines as two-sided, they put more weight on an 
upside scenario where intensified shipping attacks, damage to oil infrastructure, and a 
more persistent risk premium push oil prices up to $132/bbl (Exhibit 7). If these risks 
materialize, the energy price impulse would remain a moderate drag on global GDP 
growth in 2027.

2. Bond Market Turbulence 
Second, we worry about a bigger fiscal risk premium. In 2026, we saw the consequences 
of large-scale deficits—coupled with AI-linked borrowing and central bank hikes—show 
up more clearly in higher longer-term interest rates. Although rates have eased 
somewhat over the last few weeks, the DM yield curve remains 85bp above its level in 
early 2026. If sustained, we estimate that this increase would subtract a cumulative 0.5% 
from GDP via weakness in housing, consumer durables, and business investment (Exhibit 
8). Only part of this drag is incorporated in our standing forecasts because we expect 
bond yields to pull back as central banks deliver fewer hikes than priced in markets.

3. A Weaker AI Impulse
The transition to an AI-enabled economy is occurring in two phases. The first phase
involves an investment boom that raises both corporate profits and global GDP growth
but features only limited productivity gains and labor market disruption (because it
takes firms several years to adopt AI and reorganize to take full advantage of the new
technology). The second phase involves lower hardware investment but more visible
business reorganization, as well as more substantial productivity gains and labor market

disruption.
To illustrate this transition, we overlay a simulated AI capex path with our estimates of
the labor market impacts under a 10-year adoption timeline (Exhibit 10). The capex
path assumes that US AI investment follows equity analysts’ projections through 2032,
after which the capital stock converges toward a long-run equilibrium level.1  Under our
baseline calibration, the AI capital stock eventually converges to a long-run level of
around 9% of GDP, and 9% of total US workers are displaced. (We continue to expect

most of these displaced workers to find new jobs, including in new occupations that are
created by AI).
Under these assumptions, the transition from the first phase to the second phase
doesn’t occur until 2028-2029, when we see a further pickup in the underlying US
productivity trend to the 2½-3% range and a gradual increase in the US unemployment
rate to 4½%. A backloaded transition is consistent with the projections by equity
analysts that AI investment will keep growing through 2028, albeit at a slower pace than

in 2026-2027. It is also consistent with the renewed pickup in US employment growth
since the spring, which has temporarily laid to rest concerns around imminent AI-related
job market disruption.
1 We characterize the long-run equilibrium level following Hall and Jorgenson (1967) as a function of the
depreciation rate, technological cost disinflation, nominal interest rates, and the AI capital factor share of total
output.
12 November 2026   9
Goldman Sachs Global Economics Analyst

### Page 10

But it is hard to be sure about the timing of this shift and we see some signs that it could 
occur earlier.  Hyperscaler equity price reactions to earnings call signals for higher capex 
switched from positive in 2024-2025 to slightly negative in 2026 as the market has 
become concerned about overinvestment (Exhibit 11). Furthermore, upward revisions to 
hyperscaler capex slowed in the Q3 earnings season, which suggests that the AI 
investment cycle peak is shifting more clearly into view.

### Page 11

Beyond the fundamental demand for compute, we see three other near-term downside 
risks for AI investment: 
Capacity constraints – Semiconductor and memory producers are capacity n
constrained with order backlogs through 2028. While capacity investments currently 
underway should eventually alleviate these constraints, a surge in nominal 
investment in 2027 may increasingly raise prices (thereby pushing central banks 
toward more hawkish policy) without boosting real output. 
Credit markets – Global AI-related debt issuance has totaled $687bn so far in 2026 n
while AI-related equity issuance has added another $115bn. While our market 
strategists see adequate capital supply to meet even higher capital demand next 
year, upward pressure on borrowing costs could lead to more meaningful crowd-out 
of other investment in 2027 (vs. only modest crowd-out in 2026). 
Regulation – Concerns regarding regulatory headwinds to investment surged after n
the US midterm elections in early November raised the possibility of data center 
permitting moratoria in several states (particularly in Texas). We do not anticipate 
that regulation will derail 2027 investment, both because moratoria will likely be 
short-lived and because permits for 2027 projects have mostly already been issued. 
But a more extended delay or stringent regulatory shift would raise downside risks to 
AI investment in 2028-2029, which could weigh on growth in 2027 via tighter 
financial conditions. 
Turning to the labor market, we have argued that AI adoption is likely to displace jobs 
and cause a temporary increase in the unemployment rate of around ½ a percentage 
point over the next several years.2  Our baseline expectation is that this increase occurs 
in a backloaded fashion, with only a small effect in 2027 and more notable effects in 
2028-2029.  However, we are starting to see signs that the AI impact is spreading 
2  See “Revisiting the Extent and Timing of AI Labor Displacement,” Global Economics Analyst, October 22, 
2026.

beyond technology to the broader economy. Across nonmanufacturing industries, the 
share of US firms that have adopted AI in regular business functions now shows a 
statistically significant negative correlation with employment growth and a statistically 
significant positive correlation with unemployment (correlations were insignificant at the 
start of the year, Exhibit 12).

Moreover, AI-driven job displacement would likely accelerate in a downside growth 
scenario. Exhibit 13 shows that the trend decline in the employment share of routine 
occupations over the past five decades has been highly concentrated in recessions.  
Given that AI has sharply increased the number of occupations exposed to automation, 
we would expect corporate leaders to respond to a slowdown by reducing headcount 
targets more aggressively, putting more upward pressure on the unemployment rate.

### Page 13

To be clear, we still expect AI-related pressure on the labor market to remain temporary.  
Employment in existing industries such as healthcare is likely to keep growing rapidly, 
and history shows that technological revolutions ultimately result in the creation of new 
industries and occupations that absorb workers displaced elsewhere.  Historically, this 
dynamism has kept the unemployment rate from increasing permanently despite all the 
labor-saving technological innovations of the past 250 years.  But over the next few 
years, job market disruption is likely to grow.  
Inflation: Progress Not Perfection 
Inflation progress was slower than expected in 2026 owing to price increases associated 
with the war in Iran.  But under our baseline commodity forecasts for a steady decline in 
energy prices next year, inflation should slow in 2027. 
The case for disinflation is particularly strong in the US because the price boosts from 
tariffs (which are still adding 40bp to year-over-year core PCE inflation through 
October) should fully drop out of the year-over-year inflation statistics next spring 
(Exhibit 14). Combined with a much smaller inflation boost from the war in Iran, we 
expect core PCE to slow from 3.2% at end-2026 to 2.4% at end-2027.

Exhibit 13: Prior Automation Cycles Suggest That Aggregate Automation Speeds Up in

### Page 14

Despite our expectation for progress, we see several reasons why inflation may remain 
somewhat firm in early 2027, thereby delaying a return to target-consistent levels in 
most economies. 
First, while most of the price boost from higher energy prices has been realized, some 
pass-through will spill into next year (Exhibit 15).  This is true both in the US and 
especially in Europe, where indirect effects have so far been very limited.  For example, 
European airfares have not repriced higher in response to fuel cost increases so far—
reflecting fuel hedges, greater competition, and pre-war plans for supply 
expansion—but should pick up notably next year.

### Page 15

Second, global memory price forecasts were again revised higher over the last earnings 
season on the back of robust demand (Exhibit 16).  This pattern both suggests more 
near-term upside to core inflation from AI spillovers—although we still anticipate price 
increases should slow as production capacity increases—and points to upside risks if 
recent pricing trends continue.  This is especially true in the US, where the impact from 
higher memory prices is exaggerated because the weight of the software and 
accessories category is overstated.

Third, upstream cost pressures have increased and broadened (Exhibit 17). These 
increases are accounted for in our baseline forecasts, but historical patterns suggest that 
the recent rise in upstream cost pressures will keep inflation surprise risks elevated 
through the spring. We are particularly concerned about a reacceleration in the US, 
where the “January effect” has driven a seasonal inflation pickup almost every year since 
the pandemic (our forecast incorporates a 0.1pp boost to January core PCE).

We still see most of these price pressures as backward-looking and temporary, however, 
and remain less worried about a sustained inflation overshoot.  We take particular 
comfort in the downward trend in wage growth in most major economies that lowers the 
risk of a pickup in core services inflation (Exhibit 18).  Furthermore, leading indicators 
continue to point toward an incremental softening in US shelter inflation, and we have 
yet to see signs of second-round effects in Europe.

Taken together, we forecast a steady decline in year-over-year core inflation in the US 
and a more delayed decline in other major economies once the energy price shock is 
fully digested (Exhibit 19).  And although core inflation remains somewhat above target 
at end-2027 on a year-over-year basis, we expect that the inflation constraint will

increasingly become less binding in most economies as sequential inflation eases over 
the course of the year.

The largest risk to our near-term inflation view is that any renewed escalation in Iran 
could prolong headline (and to some degree core) inflation pressures.  Applying our 
standard rules of thumb to our commodity team’s adverse scenario implies 0.9pp upside 
to headline inflation and 0.2pp upside to core inflation relative to our baseline forecasts 
for 2027. 
Still Expecting Fewer Hikes Than Priced 
We forecast incremental policy tightening through early 2027 in most major economies 
(and see a low bar for modest incremental tightening in others), but our economic 
forecasts support a dovish view on rates in 2027: central banks could either deliver 
gradual normalization cuts later in the year as inflation comes down or pivot to cuts 
sooner if the downside growth risks discussed above materialize. 
In the US, we expect the Fed to deliver rate hikes in December and March in response to 
lingering inflation pressures (Exhibit 20).  But softer inflation data should allow the Fed 
to cut once in December 2027, followed by three more cuts in 2028 to a terminal rate of 
3.25-3.5%.  Furthermore, we see more dovish rate risks in the US than in most other 
economies, both because US inflation drivers look more clearly temporary and because 
the US is most exposed to a downturn in the AI cycle.

### Page 18

Our views on other major DMs also skew dovish (Exhibit 21). We expect the ECB to 
deliver its final hike in December, before starting to normalize back toward a 2.25% 
terminal rate in 2027Q4. And although higher-than-expected inflation could prompt 
another hike in Q1, our softer European growth outlook limits policy rate upside. In the 
UK, we expect a final BoE hike to 4.25% in February before a hold for the remainder of 
2027. We likewise expect normalization cuts from the BoE to a 3.25% terminal rate, but 
not until 2028. Our ECB and BoE forecasts are dovish relative to market pricing on both a 
baseline and a risk-adjusted basis. 
We are also dovish on Canada and Australia. In Canada, we expect two hikes to 3.0% in 
the first half of next year based on recent BoC communication suggesting it plans to 
return to the center of its neutral range (which we believe will be revised up to 2.5-3.5% 
in April).  But with trade uncertainty looming large, it is more likely that the BoC 
underdelivers than hikes above the midpoint of its neutral range (as the market is 
pricing).  In Australia, our RBA forecast for a hold at 4.85% through October is broadly in 
line with market pricing over 2027H1, but more dovish in 2027H2 and especially by 
end-2028 (by which time we expect a cumulative 125bp in cuts to have been delivered).  
The main outlier to our forecast of only modest further policy tightening is Japan. We 
expect the BoJ to deliver another 25bp hike at the December MPM, reflecting recent 
BoJ communication highlighting both upside inflation risks and the impact of higher 
global interest rates on the Yen. But with core CPI (ex fresh food but including energy) 
likely to rise above 3.5% through next summer, we now expect that the BoJ will follow 
through with additional hikes in March and June for a 2.0% terminal rate.

Our views also skew dovish in EMs (Exhibit 22). This is particularly true in CEEMEA and 
LatAm, where we expect most central banks to cut through 2027.  In Asia, we expect 
rates to stay flat or rise slightly (though our forecasts are still below market pricing 
except in Taiwan). Energy prices are the main upside risk to EM rates, but we think 
markets overestimate the inflation risk in most EMs for three reasons. First, inflation was 
already low before the energy shock. Second, EM economies are cyclically weaker than 
DMs. Third, EM currencies have held up well, supported by healthy external balances. 
Some EMs may tighten further over the next few quarters, but we expect most to hold or 
cut after that.

### Page 20

More Challenges for Markets than the Macro
As with the economic outlook, the market outlook continues to be dominated by the
path of the AI cycle and energy risks related to the Iran war.
Our baseline forecast is quite friendly. The mix of steady growth, falling core inflation,
gradually moderating energy prices and an earlier peak in G10 hiking cycles than market
pricing does not signal obvious threats to asset markets. The clearest difference with

market pricing is our more benign view of the policy rate path for many economies,
which all else equal should prove a boost to both equities and bonds. Our central asset
forecasts look for positive returns across the major asset classes. After the recent
sell-off, our forecasts imply robust equity returns between now and the end of 2027, but
we expect the pace of gains to slow as we move through next year.
Despite this baseline, we think the market outlook will continue to prove challenging, as

it has been already for several months. In part, this is because of the risks around our
baseline that we have already described. In part, it is because we think markets—
especially around the AI theme—have been priced in ways that leave them more
vulnerable to signs that important tailwinds may be starting to fade. In the process, the
volatility and rotations that have mostly remained under the surface of major equity and
credit markets this year could spill over more into shifts at the index level.

Managing the Peak in the AI Investment Cycle
The biggest uncertainties—as with the economic outlook—lie around the AI cycle. With
AI investment growth expected to remain robust, the peak in the AI investment impulse
should be a manageable event for the economy. But it is already proving to be a bigger
test for markets for three reasons. First, the AI impulse has been a much larger
contributor to growth in earnings, market value and capital spending than to growth in

GDP. Second, AI-related equities have taken credit for significant macro productivity
gains, especially companies benefiting directly from the infrastructure boom, that have
made them more vulnerable to any signs that we are on a less optimistic path. Third, the
point at which growth rates peak (a “second derivative” turning point) often matters,
even if underlying growth rates (the “first derivative”) remain healthy. Moving through
that peak allows markets to place limits on the upside of an uncertain distribution and as

growth decelerates, the period of positive surprises to expectations often ends too, as
we may be starting to see for the AI capex cycle.
12 November 2026   20
Goldman Sachs Global Economics Analyst

### Page 21

Markets have already been reflecting these concerns. Since June, we have seen a 
significant derating of companies levered to the AI infrastructure theme, which are most 
sensitive to shifts in the investment outlook and had been the biggest market 
beneficiaries in the first half of 2026. The good news is that robust profits, and a rising 
share of income going to listed companies, have helped keep both the imbalances and 
the deep valuation excesses that we saw in the late 1990s at bay. The key question in this 
cycle is whether the market is correctly assessing how much of the recent acceleration in 
profits, which has taken S&P 500 earnings to a record high relative to GDP, will prove 
persistent especially as the investment cycle itself fades. Without the capital inflows 
from the EM crises in 1997 and 1998—or the rate cuts that goods price deflation 
allowed—increased financing needs have also been putting clearer upward pressure on 
the cost of capital.

### Page 22

A Flatter Trend, but Risks of a Bumpier Transition 
Two things give us comfort in terms of how bumpy this transition will prove to be. The 
first is that although the investment impulse from AI is poised to peak, growth in both AI 
investment and earnings should remain firmly positive in 2027, especially earlier in the 
year. We expect S&P 500 earnings growth to decelerate from 36% in 2026 to a 
still-robust 11% in 2027. This may help to limit the de-rating of even the most directly 
affected areas and should reduce the impact on broader growth-sensitive assets. This is 
particularly true since recent anxiety about AI-related returns has pushed the valuations 
of many technology companies lower. The second is that the baton may be passed from 
the direct beneficiaries of the AI investment boom to other areas of the market. This 
includes other areas of the AI complex, such as the hyperscalers, if monetization paths 
become more visible; companies which may begin to show benefits from AI productivity 
enhancements; or non-AI companies which see some relief as the crowding out of 
capital available to them eases (and are now outperforming the broad AI complex in 
2026). 
The risk is that what we expect to be an eventual flattening in the upward trend in asset 
markets—and a rotation away from some of the largest recent winners—becomes a 
bigger aggregate shock. This could happen because the market begins to look further 
forward to the peak in the investment share itself (in 2028 on our forecasts); because 
the passing of the baton to other sectors does not take place as quickly or as smoothly 
as needed; because a fresh energy spike puts non-AI areas back under pressure; or 
because feedback loops from financial and credit conditions accelerate more quickly 
than in our baseline. We think the risk to markets is higher than the risk to the economic 
picture, because the boom itself has had its largest impact there. But tighter financial 
conditions—both in equities and rates—are the most obvious channel through which the 
economic impact could also increase.

### Page 23

A Gradual Shift from Rate Risk to Growth Risk
Our inflation and policy views remain more distinctive than our growth views. With our
central bank views generally more dovish than the markets, particularly as we move
deeper into 2027, our baseline forecast implies scope for front-end pricing to fall
further. The mix of high fiscal deficits and the ongoing AI capex boom limit the scope for
longer-dated yields to fall in our baseline. But if policy rates peak at the lower levels we

expect, forward rates should also move lower (we see the US 10-year yield at 4.6% at
the end of 2027). Faster energy relief would accentuate that process.
This kind of policy relief would be helpful for both equities and bonds. But shifts in the
nature of the risks facing asset markets may be starting to alter the balance of risks
between equities and bonds, and correlations across asset markets, as we move into
2027. Through much of 2026, global bonds failed to protect equity portfolios. The

inflationary impulse from energy disruptions and the surprising resilience of growth and
labor markets in the face of those shocks prompted a sharp shift in policy rate
expectations. The start of a broad-based G10 hiking cycle has pushed real rates sharply
higher in the last 9 months. Under the surface, the mix of rising corporate sector
financing needs with still-high government deficits helped to underpin the upward
pressure on global real yields.

In the near term, the key risks to our baseline economic forecasts still push in that
direction. The most obvious is that any escalation in the Iran war could push energy
prices sharply higher again in the coming months. Particularly after the fall in the risk
premium in oil markets coming into November, a fresh energy shock would hurt equities,
support the US dollar and put renewed upward pressure on rates markets. Recent
firmness in core inflation, and Fed commentary, are also likely to keep worries about a

more front-loaded hiking cycle alive. The impulse from AI investment is also arguably
moving through its strongest point, which may limit any decline in yields.
But the further we move into 2027, the more likely it is that the key risks are positive for
bonds, as the threats from central bank hikes, energy price spikes and the AI investment
tailwind recede and other risks become more prominent. A sharper reversal in the AI
boom or earlier AI-related labor market disruption are the most obvious versions of that

story. But the kinds of financial stresses from a high-rate environment that we saw
emerge in September and October—most obviously in France heading towards the April
election but potentially also elsewhere—would push in a similar direction, as would
“black swan” risks such as a major cybersecurity event.
12 November 2026   23
Goldman Sachs Global Economics Analyst

### Page 24

Diversification and Protection as the Market Cycle Matures 
With a steady economic backdrop, our baseline forecasts for 2027 are for positive 
returns in equities and bonds, and close to zero returns in commodities. But with a 
maturing market cycle and significant uncertainty around core drivers of the outlook, we 
continue to emphasize the value of diversification and hedges to protect against key 
risks.

We expect ongoing earnings growth alongside the recent valuation compression to 
support further gains in major equity markets in 2027. As the pace of earnings growth 
slows moving into 2027, the pace of US equity gains is likely to drop too, moving well 
below the more than 20% annualized rate of the last three years. With earnings growth

### Page 25

likely to be slower still in 2028, the risk is that the market pulls forward its worries about
a deeper turn in the cycle. Accounting for risks around the baseline view, we recommend
an overweight allocation in the US and Japan, neutral Asia ex Japan/EM, and
underweight in Europe.
We expect further widening in credit spreads in 2027 as the increase in corporate supply
continues. Although widening is modest in our base case, risks are clearly skewed in that

direction. It is hard to see scenarios where US credit spreads tighten sharply (there is
more scope for tightening in Europe where the risk premium has risen more), and easier
to see how spreads could move higher, particularly if economic conditions deteriorate
more than we expect or if investors become more uncomfortable about the profitability
of the AI investment boom. We expect US HY to underperform, with some risk that
problems in the tail of weaker borrowers create more turbulent conditions for broader

financing. Without the large capital inflows and rate cuts prompted by the EM crises in
1997-98, the sustained divergence between credit and equity markets from 1998 to
2000 may more challenging now.
Government bonds offer better returns than in recent years, reflecting higher yields and
improving relative valuations. Real bond yields are at high levels compared with the last
30 years, both absolutely and relative to equities.  Our baseline is for bonds to deliver a

little more than the decent running yield that they currently offer, though the scope for
capital gains is likely to be higher later in 2027. There are risks to that view from fresh
energy shocks, or stickier core inflation than we expect. But if clearer downside risks to
growth emerge, yields could fall more clearly.
After tight ranges in 2026, our baseline is for modest USD weakness as lower inflation
and energy put the USD on the back foot, alongside further appreciation in CNY

underpinned by the surging trade surplus. But if the market challenges US tech
exceptionalism more quickly, we could see a sharper shift away from the USD. And the
same set of growth risks that are increasing the hedging value of bonds would also likely
prompt a sharper appreciation in the Japanese Yen.
Commodities continue to offer value as a portfolio hedge. Despite the ongoing war with
Iran, oil market supply has largely been restored, but risks of escalation and further

disruptions have led to a large but variable risk premium above the oil price justified by
the fundamental supply-demand balance. Our baseline is that this risk premium will
slowly erode, leaving energy and core commodity returns more muted. But there is still
heightened risk of scenarios where energy prices could move much higher, hurting
bonds and equities. While there is also a downside tail to energy prices, if there is faster
progress towards a resolution of the war with Iran, in that scenario equities and bonds

would likely be performing well.
12 November 2026   25
Goldman Sachs Global Economics Analyst

### Page 26

Alongside diversification, direct hedges are worth considering. Positioning for lower 
rates in the US and Japan, or for jointly lower rates and equities are potential ways to 
protect against deeper downside growth tails. In FX, USD/JPY downside may play a 
similar role, given that it generally performs well when rates and equities are both falling. 
For energy upside, hedges in European gas futures and TTF calls look like asymmetric 
ways to protect against risk. So far, the sharpest volatility has been under the surface of 
the major equity indices. Equity index volatility has risen a little but is unlikely to fall 
much from here and we think has scope to rise further if macro stresses broaden. 
Deeper protection in equities themselves—either through out-of-the-money puts or 
replacing equity holdings with longer-dated call options—also seems more valuable 
than usual as the cycle matures.

## Document 333: Global Views_ Downside Growth Risks

Source PDF: all report pdf/Global Views_ Downside Growth Risks.pdf
Report source: Goldman Sachs Publishing
Date: 2026-10-26
Pages: 10
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Jan Hatzius 
+1(212)902-0394 | jan.hatzius@gs.com 
Goldman Sachs & Co. LLC
Global Views: Downside Growth Risks
26 October 2026 | 6:45AM EDT
1. So far in 2026, global growth has been remarkably unremarkable.  Both the world 
as a whole and the G3 economies are growing in line with our estimates of potential 
GDP.  Our interpretation is that fiscal easing and the positive impulse from the AI 
boom have offset the negative impulse from the war in Iran and the lingering drag 
from higher US tariffs.

2. Our standing global GDP forecasts project more of the same in 2027, as lower 
energy prices offset fading tailwinds from fiscal policy and AI.  But the balance of 
risks is on the downside.  The most immediate concern is that, far from subsiding, 
energy prices have again climbed sharply on fears of renewed Middle East escalation.  
The prices of gasoline, diesel, jet fuel, and European/Asian natural gas are now near 
or even above their March/April highs, with likely negative effects on consumers and 
energy-importing economies more broadly.

### Page 2

3. Although we don’t expect significant fiscal policy changes in 2027, the consequences 
of large-scale deficits—coupled with AI-linked borrowing and central bank hikes—have 
started to show up more clearly in higher longer-term interest rates.  The moves have 
been mostly orderly, but the increase since early 2026 averages a sizable 100bp across 
DM yield curves.  If sustained, we estimate that this increase will subtract a cumulative 
0.6% from GDP via weakness in housing, consumer durables, and business investment.  
(Only part of the drag is incorporated in our standing forecasts because we expect bond 
yields to pull back as central banks deliver fewer hikes than priced in markets.)

Exhibit 2: Oil Price Risks Are Skewed to the Upside

Source: Goldman Sachs Global Investment Research
26 October 2026   2
Goldman Sachs Global Views

### Page 3

4. The growth impulse from the AI boom was set to peak in late 2026 or early 2027 even 
before concerns around the sustainability of the AI investment boom intensified in 
recent weeks.  Although AI investment is still rising rapidly, we expect the increase as a 
share of GDP to slow over the next year.  Alongside the fading boost from last year’s 
increase in US depreciation allowances, this will weigh on capital spending growth, 
especially in the US.  AI has also boosted US consumer spending growth via a positive 
wealth effect worth an estimated 0.4pp (or 0.25% of GDP) over the past year.  This 
impact is likely to decline over the next year, gradually if stocks perform in line with our 
strategists’ reasonably upbeat expectations but more sharply if AI-related stocks 
continue to decline.

5. Our own base case remains that AI investment will ultimately pay off in terms of higher 
productivity and economywide profits.  However, one key channel for this payoff is labor 
market disruption, which has negative near-term macroeconomic effects.  And while DM 
labor markets still look solid in aggregate, we are starting to see clearer signs that the AI 
impact is spreading beyond the technology sector.  Across nonmanufacturing industries, 
the share of US firms that have adopted AI in regular business functions now shows a 
statistically significant negative correlation with employment growth and a statistically 
significant positive correlation with unemployment.

6. The Fed’s recent signals—explicit from Williams/Jefferson and implicit from Warsh—
effectively rule out a hike on October 28.  But the FOMC still looks very likely to deliver 
another 25bp hike to 4-4.25% on December 9 because spot inflation remains too high.  
And while we continue to expect significant disinflation next year on the back of 
reduced impulses from tariffs, energy prices, and AI-related prices, the risks to our 
forecast are on the upside, most obviously in energy.  Higher inflation could result in

another hike in March, but either way we see renewed cuts in late 2027/early 2028.

7. Like the Fed, the ECB will likely hold rates steady next week and deliver a final hike (to 
2.75%) at the December meeting.  However, the turbulence in the French bond market 
poses somewhat greater downside risks to that call than in the US.  The turbulence has 
tightened financial conditions to a degree that is likely to shave ¼-½pp from annualized 
growth in coming quarters.  And if it intensifies into December, markets might perceive a 
hike as signaling insufficient focus by the ECB on the risk of Euro area fragmentation.  
While the ECB could address this issue by coupling a hike with renewed balance sheet 
expansion, the hurdle for such a move is high, in part because purchases of French 
bonds would inevitably raise concerns about moral hazard with respect to the needed 
fiscal adjustment.
